     1→# Multi-stage Dockerfile for moltis
     2→# Builds a minimal debian-based image with the moltis gateway
     3→#
     4→# Moltis uses Docker/Podman for sandboxed command execution. To enable this,
     5→# mount the container runtime socket when running:

     8→#   Podman:    -v /run/podman/podman.sock:/var/run/docker.sock

    10→#
    11→# See README.md for detailed instructions.

    13→# Build stage — nightly required for wacore-binary (portable_simd)
    14→FROM rust:bookworm AS builder
    15→
    16→WORKDIR /build
    17→
    18→# Switch to nightly (pinned for reproducibility; wacore-binary needs portable_simd)

# Forum topic session isolation
### Problem
Distinct Telegram chats were already isolated upstream by chat_id, but forum topics inside the same Telegram supergroup were not. They shared the same session key and the same interrupt bucket, which caused topic context bleed and cross-topic interruption. The tool-side target directory also collapsed topics down to the parent chat, so explicit topic sends were not possible.

- include Telegram thread_id in non-DM session keys so forum topics inside one supergroup do not share context
- key active gateway interrupts by the canonical session key instead of raw chat_id
- preserve Telegram topic routing for replies, cron origin delivery, transcript mirroring, and tool-based sends
- expose Telegram forum topics as distinct send_message targets and support explicit telegram:chat_id:thread_id addressing

Basically: Is it possible to segment/isolate forum/group topics so we can have specific agents/models/memory/sessions exclusive to that particular topic?

---COMMENTS---
```

Is `thread_id` included?\n2. How are interrupts keyed — by `chat_id` or by a canonical session key?\n3. How are replies routed back to specific forum topics (thread_id preservation)?\n4. Can `send_message` target a specific forum topic (e.g. `telegram:chat_id:thread_id`)?\n\nSearch across `crates/` for:\n- `thread_id` usage in Telegram-related code\n- Session key construction (look for `session_key`, `SessionKey`, or similar)\n- Interrupt bucketing\n- `send_message` target parsing for Telegram\n\nBe thorough — check telegram handler code, session management, gateway chat service, and any tool that sends messages."
}

Perfect! Now I have enough information. Let me create a comprehensive summary document based on my findings:

## Summary: Telegram Forum Topics and Session Isolation

Based on my thorough search of the codebase, here are the answers to your questions:

### 1. **Session Key Construction**

The session key for Telegram messages **does NOT include `thread_id` (forum topics)**. 

**Current implementation** (from `crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs`, line 23-27):
```rust
fn default_channel_session_key(target: &ChannelReplyTarget) -> String {
    format!(
        "{}:{}:{}",
        target.channel_type, target.account_id, target.chat_id
    )
}
```

This produces keys like: `telegram:bot_account:12345` where only the `chat_id` is used, NOT the forum topic ID.

**Note:** There is a dead-code `build_session_key()` function in `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/oasis-cattle/crates/telegram/src/handlers.rs` (line 1809-1822) that follows the same pattern:
- DM: `telegram:bot1:dm:user123`
- Group/Channel: `telegram:bot1:group:-100999`

The function is marked `#[allow(dead_code)]` and is only called in tests.

### 2. **Interrupts Keying**

Interrupts are keyed by **`session_key` only** (which is derived from `channel_type:account_id:chat_id`), **NOT by individual messages**.

**Evidence** (from `crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs`, line 1656):
```rust
"stop" => {
    let params = serde_json::json!({ "sessionKey": session_key });
    match chat.abort(params).await {
```

And in `crates/chat/src/lib.rs` (line 4127-4145):
```rust
async fn abort(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult {
    let run_id = params.get("runId").and_then(|v| v.as_str());
    let session_key = params.get("sessionKey").and_then(|v| v.as_str());
    if run_id.is_none() && session_key.is_none() {
        return Err("missing 'runId' or 'sessionKey'".into());
    }
    // ... abort the entire session OR specific run
}
```

So interrupts affect **all runs in a session**, not individual forum topics.

### 3. **Reply Routing and Thread Preservation**

Reply routing **preserves `message_id` for threading**, but **not forum topics** (thread_id).

**Evidence** (from `crates/telegram/src/handlers.rs`, line 555-560):
```rust
let reply_target = ChannelReplyTarget {
    channel_type: ChannelType::Telegram,
    account_id: account_id.to_string(),
    chat_id: msg.chat.id.0.to_string(),
    message_id: Some(msg.id.0.to_string()),
};
```

The `ChannelReplyTarget` structure (from `crates/channels/src/plugin.rs`, line 443-451):
```rust
pub struct ChannelReplyTarget {
    pub channel_type: ChannelType,
    pub account_id: String,
    /// Chat/peer ID to send the reply to.
    pub chat_id: String,
    /// Platform-specific message ID of the inbound message.
    /// Used to thread replies (e.g. Telegram `reply_to_message_id`).
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub message_id: Option<String>,
}
```

The `message_id` is used for Telegram's `reply_to_message_id` field, which threads replies to a specific message but **does NOT isolate forum topics**.

### 4. **`send_message` Target Parsing**

The `send_message` tool **does NOT support targeting specific forum topics** via the target syntax.

**Evidence** (from `crates/gateway/src/channel.rs`, line 382-447):

The `send()` method accepts:
- `account_id` (required) - Channel account identifier
- `to` (required) - Destination chat_id (aliases: `chat_id`, `chatId`, `peer_id`, `peerId`)
- `reply_to` (optional) - Platform message_id for threading (aliases: `replyTo`, `message_id`, `messageId`)

**The `to` parameter accepts only `chat_id`, not `chat_id:thread_id`.**

Example usage:
```json
{
  "account_id": "my_bot",
  "to": "12345",
  "text": "Hello",
  "reply_to": "999"
}
```

Where:
- `to: "12345"` targets the chat/group
- `reply_to: "999"` threads to message 999 (but doesn't isolate to a forum topic)

### **Key Gap: No Forum Topic Isolation**

The codebase currently **does not support forum topic isolation**. All messages in a Telegram group are keyed by `chat_id` alone, regardless of which forum topic they're in. This means:

1. If you send multiple messages to different forum topics in the same group, they **all use the same session**
2. The `/stop` command stops the agent for **all forum topics in the group**
3. The LLM sees **all messages from all topics mixed together**

To support forum topics, you would need to:
1. Include `message_thread_id` in the session key construction
2. Extract `message_thread_id` from incoming Telegram messages (available in teloxide's `Message::message_thread_id`)
3. Pass forum topic ID through the reply target structure to `send_message`

> AGENT

Good findings. The discussion is essentially a feature request, and the answer is clear from the code.

**Current state:** Forum topics inside a Telegram supergroup are **not isolated**. The session key is built from `channel_type:account_id:chat_id` only — `thread_id` is not included. This means:

- All topics in a group **share the same session** (context bleed)
- Interrupts (`/stop`) affect **all topics** in the group
- `send_message` can't target a specific topic (only `chat_id`, no `thread_id` support)
- Reply threading uses `reply_to_message_id` but that's per-message, not per-topic

**What would need to change** to support this:

1. **Session key** — include `message_thread_id` in `default_channel_session_key()` for non-DM Telegram messages (`crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs`)
2. **ChannelReplyTarget** — add an optional `thread_id` field (`crates/channels/src/plugin.rs`) and populate it from `msg.message_thread_id` in the Telegram handler
3. **Interrupt bucketing** — already keyed by session_key, so fixing (1) fixes this automatically
4. **send_message tool** — support `telegram:chat_id:thread_id` addressing in the channel send path
5. **Cron/transcript routing** — thread the topic ID through cron origin delivery and transcript mirroring

It's a real gap and a reasonable feature request. Want me to reply to the discussion with this analysis, create a beads issue to track it, or start implementing?

### 10. **ChannelReplyTarget Structure Definition**

**Definition** (`crates/channels/src/plugin.rs` lines 443-450):
```rust
pub struct ChannelReplyTarget {
    pub channel_type: ChannelType,
    pub account_id: String,
    pub chat_id: String,

}
```

---

## Summary of Required Changes:

### For Forum Topic Session Isolation:

1. **Extend `ChannelReplyTarget`** to include `message_thread_id: Option<String>` field
2. **Update `default_channel_session_key`** to include message_thread_id in key: `"{}:{}:{}:{}"` format
3. **Update `channel_sessions` table** schema to add `message_thread_id TEXT` column to the PRIMARY KEY
4. **Update `get_active_session` & `set_active_session`** to accept and query by message_thread_id
5. **Update all 11 file locations** where `ChannelReplyTarget` is constructed to extract and include `message_thread_id`
6. **Update Telegram send functions** (`send_context_card`, `send_agent_keyboard`, `send_sessions_keyboard`) to accept and use `message_thread_id`
7. **Update `maybe_deliver_cron_output`** to support forum topics (likely needs AgentTurnRequest extension)
8. **Update Cron types** to include optional `message_thread_id` field
9. **Update serialization/deserialization** of ChannelReplyTarget throughout chat binding code
10. **Update all test fixtures** (3 locations) to include message_thread_id

All absolute file paths have been provided above.

   433→impl ChannelOutbound for TelegramOutbound {
   434→    async fn send_text(
   435→        &self,
   436→        account_id: &str,

   445→        // Send typing indicator

   447→
   448→        let chunks = markdown::chunk_markdown_html(text, TELEGRAM_MAX_MESSAGE_LEN);
   449→        info!(
   450→            account_id,
   451→            chat_id = to,
   452→            reply_to = ?reply_to,
   453→            text_len = text.len(),
   454→            chunk_count = chunks.len(),
   455→            "telegram outbound text send start"
   456→        );
   457→
   458→        for chunk in chunks.iter() {
   459→            let reply_params = rp.as_ref();
   460→            self.send_chunk_with_fallback(
   461→                &bot,
   462→                account_id,
   463→                to,
   464→                chat_id,
   465→                chunk,
   466→                reply_params,
   467→                false,
   468→            )
   469→            .await?;
   470→        }
   471→
   472→        info!(
   473→            account_id,
   474→            chat_id = to,
   475→            reply_to = ?reply_to,
   476→            text_len = text.len(),
   477→            chunk_count = chunks.len(),
   478→            "telegram outbound text sent"
   479→        );
   480→        Ok(())
   481→    }
   482→
   483→    async fn send_text_with_suffix(
   484→        &self,
   485→        account_id: &str,
   486→        to: &str,
   487→        text: &str,
   488→        suffix_html: &str,
   489→        reply_to: Option<&str>,
   490→    ) -> Result<()> {
   491→        let bot = self.get_bot(account_id)?;
   492→        let chat_id = ChatId(to.parse::<i64>()?);
   493→        let rp = self.reply_params(account_id, reply_to);
   494→
   495→        // Send typing indicator
   496→        let _ = bot.send_chat_action(chat_id, ChatAction::Typing).await;
   497→
   498→        // Append the pre-formatted suffix (e.g. activity logbook) to the last chunk.
   499→        let chunks = markdown::chunk_markdown_html(text, TELEGRAM_MAX_MESSAGE_LEN);
   500→        let last_idx = chunks.len().saturating_sub(1);
   501→        info!(
   502→            account_id,
   503→            chat_id = to,
   504→            reply_to = ?reply_to,
   505→            text_len = text.len(),
   506→            suffix_len = suffix_html.len(),
   507→            chunk_count = chunks.len(),
   508→            "telegram outbound text+suffix send start"
   509→        );
   510→
   511→        for (i, chunk) in chunks.iter().enumerate() {
   512→            let content = if i == last_idx {
   513→                // Append suffix to the last chunk. If it would exceed the limit,
   514→                // the suffix becomes a separate final message.
   515→                let combined = format!("{chunk}\n\n{suffix_html}");
   516→                if combined.len() <= TELEGRAM_MAX_MESSAGE_LEN {
   517→                    combined
   518→                } else {
   519→                    // Send this chunk first, then the suffix as a separate message.
   520→                    self.send_chunk_with_fallback(
   521→                        &bot,
   522→                        account_id,
   523→                        to,
   524→                        chat_id,
   525→                        chunk,
   526→                        rp.as_ref(),
   527→                        false,
   528→                    )
   529→                    .await?;
   530→                    // Send suffix as the final message (no reply threading).
   531→                    self.send_chunk_with_fallback(
   532→                        &bot,
   533→                        account_id,
   534→                        to,
   535→                        chat_id,
   536→                        suffix_html,
   537→                        rp.as_ref(),
   538→                        true,
   539→                    )
   540→                    .await?;
   541→                    info!(
   542→                        account_id,
   543→                        chat_id = to,
   544→                        reply_to = ?reply_to,
   545→                        text_len = text.len(),
   546→                        suffix_len = suffix_html.len(),
   547→                        chunk_count = chunks.len(),
   548→                        "telegram outbound text+suffix sent (separate suffix message)"
   549→                    );
   550→                    return Ok(());
   551→                }

   382→    async fn send(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult {

   385→            .or_else(|| params.get("channel"))
   386→            .and_then(|v| v.as_str())

   400→        let text = params
   401→            .get("text")
   402→            .or_else(|| params.get("message"))
   403→            .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
   404→            .map(str::trim)
   405→            .filter(|value| !value.is_empty())

   415→        let silent = params

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "/// Where to send the LLM response back.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]\npub struct ChannelReplyTarget {\n    pub channel_type: ChannelType,\n    pub account_id: String,\n    /// Chat/peer ID to send the reply to.\n    pub chat_id: String,\n    /// Platform-specific message ID of the inbound message.\n    /// Used to thread replies (e.g. Telegram `reply_to_message_id`).\n    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = \"Option::is_none\")]\n    pub message_id: Option<String>,\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Where to send the LLM response back.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]\npub struct ChannelReplyTarget {\n    pub channel_type: ChannelType,\n    pub account_id: String,\n    /// Chat/peer ID to send the reply to.\n    pub chat_id: String,\n    /// Platform-specific message ID of the inbound message.\n    /// Used to thread replies (e.g. Telegram `reply_to_message_id`).\n    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = \"Option::is_none\")]\n    pub message_id: Option<String>,\n    /// Forum-topic / thread identifier (e.g. Telegram `message_thread_id`).\n    /// When present, outbound messages are routed to this topic instead of the\n    /// top-level chat.\n    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = \"Option::is_none\")]\n    pub thread_id: Option<String>,\n}\n\nimpl ChannelReplyTarget {\n    /// Returns the address string for outbound sends.\n    ///\n    /// For Telegram forum topics this encodes both chat and thread as\n    /// `\"chat_id:thread_id\"` so the outbound implementation can route to the\n    /// correct topic. All other channels return the plain `chat_id`.\n    pub fn outbound_to(&self) -> std::borrow::Cow<'_, str> {\n        match &self.thread_id {\n            Some(tid) => std::borrow::Cow::Owned(format!(\"{}:{}\", self.chat_id, tid)),\n            None => std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(&self.chat_id),\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

  "new_string": "/// Default (deterministic) session key for a channel chat.\n///\n/// For Telegram forum topics the thread ID is appended so each topic gets its\n/// own session: `telegram:bot:chat:thread`.\nfn default_channel_session_key(target: &ChannelReplyTarget) -> String {\n    match &target.thread_id {\n        Some(tid) => format!(\n            \"{}:{}:{}:{}\",\n            target.channel_type, target.account_id, target.chat_id, tid\n        ),\n        None => format!(\n            \"{}:{}:{}\",\n            target.channel_type, target.account_id, target.chat_id\n        ),\n    }\n}"
}

AND account_id = ? AND chat_id = ?\",\n        )\n        .bind(channel_type)\n        .bind(account_id)\n        .bind(chat_id)\n        .fetch_optional(&self.pool)\n        .await\n        .ok()\n        .flatten()\n    }\n\n    /// Set (upsert) the active session key for a channel chat.\n    pub async fn set_active_session(\n        &self,\n        channel_type: &str,\n        account_id: &str,\n        chat_id: &str,\n        session_key: &str,\n    ) {\n        let now = now_ms() as i64;\n        sqlx::query(\n            r#\"INSERT INTO channel_sessions (channel_type, account_id, chat_id, session_key, updated_at)\n               VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\n               ON CONFLICT(channel_type, account_id, chat_id) DO UPDATE SET\n                 session_key = excluded.session_key,\n                 updated_at = excluded.updated_at\"#,\n        )\n        .bind(channel_type)\n        .bind(account_id)\n        .bind(chat_id)\n        .bind(session_key)\n        .bind(now)\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await\n        .ok();\n    }",
  "new_string": "    /// Get the active session key for a channel chat, if one has been explicitly set.\n    pub async fn get_active_session(\n        &self,\n        channel_type: &str,\n        account_id: &str,\n        chat_id: &str,\n        thread_id: Option<&str>,\n    ) -> Option<String> {\n        let tid = thread_id.unwrap_or(\"\");\n        sqlx::query_scalar::<_, String>(\n            \"SELECT session_key FROM channel_sessions WHERE channel_type = ? AND account_id = ? AND chat_id = ? AND thread_id = ?\",\n        )\n        .bind(channel_type)\n        .bind(account_id)\n        .bind(chat_id)\n        .bind(tid)\n        .fetch_optional(&self.pool)\n        .await\n        .ok()\n        .flatten()\n    }\n\n    /// Set (upsert) the active session key for a channel chat.\n    pub async fn set_active_session(\n        &self,\n        channel_type: &str,\n        account_id: &str,\n        chat_id: &str,\n        thread_id: Option<&str>,\n        session_key: &str,\n    ) {\n        let now = now_ms() as i64;\n        let tid = thread_id.unwrap_or(\"\");\n        sqlx::query(\n            r#\"INSERT INTO channel_sessions (channel_type, account_id, chat_id, thread_id, session_key, updated_at)\n               VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\n               ON CONFLICT(channel_type, account_id, chat_id, thread_id) DO UPDATE SET\n                 session_key = excluded.session_key,\n                 updated_at = excluded.updated_at\"#,\n        )\n        .bind(channel_type)\n        .bind(account_id)\n        .bind(chat_id)\n        .bind(tid)\n        .bind(session_key)\n        .bind(now)\n        .execute(&self.pool)\n        .await\n        .ok();\n    }"

  "content": "-- Add thread_id to channel_sessions for Telegram forum-topic session isolation.\n-- SQLite cannot alter primary keys, so recreate the table.\n\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS channel_sessions_new (\n    channel_type TEXT    NOT NULL,\n    account_id   TEXT    NOT NULL,\n    chat_id      TEXT    NOT NULL,\n    thread_id    TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT '',\n    session_key  TEXT    NOT NULL,\n    updated_at   INTEGER NOT NULL,\n    PRIMARY KEY (channel_type, account_id, chat_id, thread_id)\n);\n\nINSERT OR IGNORE INTO channel_sessions_new (channel_type, account_id, chat_id, thread_id, session_key, updated_at)\n    SELECT channel_type, account_id, chat_id, '', session_key, updated_at\n    FROM channel_sessions;\n\nDROP TABLE IF EXISTS channel_sessions;\n\nALTER TABLE channel_sessions_new RENAME TO channel_sessions;\n"
}

  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/oasis-cattle/crates/telegram/src/outbound.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Outbound message sender for Telegram.\npub struct TelegramOutbound {\n    pub(crate) accounts: AccountStateMap,\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Parse a composite `to` address into `(ChatId, Option<ThreadId>)`.\n///\n/// Forum-topic sends encode the thread as `\"chat_id:thread_id\"`.\n/// Plain sends are just `\"chat_id\"`.\nfn parse_chat_target(to: &str) -> Result<(ChatId, Option<ThreadId>)> {\n    if let Some((chat_part, thread_part)) = to.split_once(':') {\n        let chat_id = ChatId(chat_part.parse::<i64>()?);\n        let thread_id = thread_part\n            .parse::<i32>()\n            .ok()\n            .map(|id| ThreadId(MessageId(id)));\n        Ok((chat_id, thread_id))\n    } else {\n        Ok((ChatId(to.parse::<i64>()?), None))\n    }\n}\n\n/// Outbound message sender for Telegram.\npub struct TelegramOutbound {\n    pub(crate) accounts: AccountStateMap,\n}"
}

   507→
   508→    async fn send_text_with_suffix(
   509→        &self,
   510→        account_id: &str,
   511→        to: &str,
   512→        text: &str,
   513→        suffix_html: &str,
   514→        reply_to: Option<&str>,
   515→    ) -> Result<()> {
   516→        let bot = self.get_bot(account_id)?;
   517→        let chat_id = ChatId(to.parse::<i64>()?);
   518→        let rp = self.reply_params(account_id, reply_to);
   519→
   520→        // Send typing indicator
   521→        let _ = bot.send_chat_action(chat_id, ChatAction::Typing).await;
   522→
   523→        // Append the pre-formatted suffix (e.g. activity logbook) to the last chunk.
   524→        let chunks = markdown::chunk_markdown_html(text, TELEGRAM_MAX_MESSAGE_LEN);
   525→        let last_idx = chunks.len().saturating_sub(1);
   526→        info!(
   527→            account_id,
   528→            chat_id = to,
   529→            reply_to = ?reply_to,
   530→            text_len = text.len(),
   531→            suffix_len = suffix_html.len(),
   532→            chunk_count = chunks.len(),
   533→            "telegram outbound text+suffix send start"
   534→        );
   535→
   536→        for (i, chunk) in chunks.iter().enumerate() {
   537→            let content = if i == last_idx {
   538→                // Append suffix to the last chunk. If it would exceed the limit,
   539→                // the suffix becomes a separate final message.
   540→                let combined = format!("{chunk}\n\n{suffix_html}");
   541→                if combined.len() <= TELEGRAM_MAX_MESSAGE_LEN {
   542→                    combined
   543→                } else {
   544→                    // Send this chunk first, then the suffix as a separate message.
   545→                    self.send_chunk_with_fallback(
   546→                        &bot,
   547→                        account_id,
   548→                        to,
   549→                        chat_id,
   550→                        chunk,
   551→                        rp.as_ref(),
   552→                        false,
   553→                    )
   554→                    .await?;
   555→                    // Send suffix as the final message (no reply threading).
   556→                    self.send_chunk_with_fallback(
   557→                        &bot,
   558→                        account_id,
   559→                        to,
   560→                        chat_id,
   561→                        suffix_html,
   562→                        rp.as_ref(),
   563→                        true,
   564→                    )
   565→                    .await?;
   566→                    info!(
   567→                        account_id,
   568→                        chat_id = to,
   569→                        reply_to = ?reply_to,
   570→                        text_len = text.len(),
   571→                        suffix_len = suffix_html.len(),
   572→                        chunk_count = chunks.len(),
   573→                        "telegram outbound text+suffix sent (separate suffix message)"
   574→                    );
   575→                    return Ok(());
   576→                }
   577→            } else {
   578→                chunk.clone()
   579→            };

> AGENT

This uses raw `msg.chat.id.0.to_string()`. For forum topics, this should include the thread_id. Since `msg` is in scope, I can compose the `to` string. Let me add a helper in the telegram handler to compose the outbound `to`:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01GKxCUgi3qMVt1NzhRc4sp2
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/oasis-cattle/crates/telegram/src/handlers.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Extract the forum-topic thread ID from a Telegram message, if present.\nfn extract_thread_id(msg: &Message) -> Option<String> {\n    msg.thread_id.map(|tid| tid.0.0.to_string())\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Extract the forum-topic thread ID from a Telegram message, if present.\nfn extract_thread_id(msg: &Message) -> Option<String> {\n    msg.thread_id.map(|tid| tid.0.0.to_string())\n}\n\n/// Compose the outbound `to` address for a Telegram message, encoding the\n/// forum-topic thread ID when present: `\"chat_id:thread_id\"`.\nfn outbound_to_for_msg(msg: &Message) -> String {\n    match extract_thread_id(msg) {\n        Some(tid) => format!(\"{}:{}\", msg.chat.id.0, tid),\n        None => msg.chat.id.0.to_string(),\n    }\n}"
}
```

  "new_string": "/// Parse a composite `to` address into `(ChatId, Option<ThreadId>)`.\nfn parse_chat_target(to: &str) -> (ChatId, Option<ThreadId>) {\n    if let Some((chat_part, thread_part)) = to.split_once(':') {\n        let chat_id = ChatId(chat_part.parse::<i64>().unwrap_or(0));\n        let thread_id = thread_part\n            .parse::<i32>()\n            .ok()\n            .map(|id| ThreadId(MessageId(id)));\n        (chat_id, thread_id)\n    } else {\n        (ChatId(to.parse::<i64>().unwrap_or(0)), None)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Extract the forum-topic thread ID from a Telegram message, if present.\nfn extract_thread_id(msg: &Message) -> Option<String> {"
}

  1195→async fn send_context_card(bot: &Bot, chat_id: &str, context_text: &str) {
  1196→    let chat = ChatId(chat_id.parse().unwrap_or(0));
  1197→
  1198→    // Parse "**Key:** value" lines from the markdown response into a map.
  1199→    let mut fields: Vec<(&str, String)> = Vec::new();
  1200→    for line in context_text.lines() {
  1201→        let trimmed = line.trim();
  1202→        if let Some(rest) = trimmed.strip_prefix("**")
  1203→            && let Some(end) = rest.find("**")
  1204→        {
  1205→            let label = &rest[..end];
  1206→            let raw_value = rest[end + 2..].trim();
  1207→            // Strip markdown backticks from value
  1208→            let value = raw_value.replace('`', "");
  1209→            fields.push((label, escape_html_simple(&value)));
  1210→        }
  1211→    }
  1212→
  1213→    let get = |key: &str| -> String {
  1214→        fields
  1215→            .iter()
  1216→            .find(|(k, _)| *k == key)
  1217→            .map(|(_, v)| v.clone())
  1218→            .unwrap_or_default()
  1219→    };
  1220→
  1221→    let session = get("Session:");
  1222→    let messages = get("Messages:");
  1223→    let provider = get("Provider:");
  1224→    let model = get("Model:");
  1225→    let sandbox = get("Sandbox:");
  1226→    let plugins_raw = get("Plugins:");
  1227→    let tokens = get("Tokens:");
  1228→
  1229→    // Format plugins as individual lines
  1230→    let plugins_section = if plugins_raw == "none" || plugins_raw.is_empty() {
  1231→        "  <i>none</i>".to_string()
  1232→    } else {
  1233→        plugins_raw
  1234→            .split(", ")
  1235→            .map(|p| format!("  ▸ {p}"))
  1236→            .collect::<Vec<_>>()
  1237→            .join("\n")
  1238→    };
  1239→
  1240→    // Sandbox indicator
  1241→    let sandbox_icon = if sandbox.starts_with("on") {
  1242→        "🟢"
  1243→    } else {
  1244→        "⚫"

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/oasis-cattle/crates/sessions/src/metadata.rs",
  "old_string": "    async fn test_sqlite_active_session() {\n        let pool = sqlite_pool().await;\n        let meta = SqliteSessionMetadata::new(pool);\n\n        // No active session initially.\n        assert!(\n            meta.get_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\")\n                .await\n                .is_none()\n        );\n\n        // Set and get.\n        meta.set_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\", \"session:abc\")\n            .await;\n        assert_eq!(\n            meta.get_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\")\n                .await\n                .as_deref(),\n            Some(\"session:abc\")\n        );\n\n        // Overwrite.\n        meta.set_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\", \"session:def\")\n            .await;\n        assert_eq!(\n            meta.get_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\")\n                .await\n                .as_deref(),\n            Some(\"session:def\")\n        );\n\n        // Different chat_id is independent.\n        assert!(\n            meta.get_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"456\")\n                .await\n                .is_none()\n        );\n    }",
  "new_string": "    async fn test_sqlite_active_session() {\n        let pool = sqlite_pool().await;\n        let meta = SqliteSessionMetadata::new(pool);\n\n        // No active session initially.\n        assert!(\n            meta.get_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\", None)\n                .await\n                .is_none()\n        );\n\n        // Set and get.\n        meta.set_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\", None, \"session:abc\")\n            .await;\n        assert_eq!(\n            meta.get_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\", None)\n                .await\n                .as_deref(),\n            Some(\"session:abc\")\n        );\n\n        // Overwrite.\n        meta.set_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\", None, \"session:def\")\n            .await;\n        assert_eq!(\n            meta.get_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\", None)\n                .await\n                .as_deref(),\n            Some(\"session:def\")\n        );\n\n        // Different chat_id is independent.\n        assert!(\n            meta.get_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"456\", None)\n                .await\n                .is_none()\n        );\n\n        // Thread ID isolates sessions within the same chat.\n        meta.set_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\", Some(\"42\"), \"session:topic\")\n            .await;\n        assert_eq!(\n            meta.get_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\", Some(\"42\"))\n                .await\n                .as_deref(),\n            Some(\"session:topic\")\n        );\n        // Original chat without thread_id still has its own session.\n        assert_eq!(\n            meta.get_active_session(\"telegram\", \"bot1\", \"123\", None)\n                .await\n                .as_deref(),\n            Some(\"session:def\")\n        );\n    }"
}

> AGENT

All 696 tests pass across the 5 affected crates. Here's a summary of the implementation:

## Changes

**Core type** (`crates/channels/src/plugin.rs`):
- Added `thread_id: Option<String>` field to `ChannelReplyTarget`
- Added `outbound_to()` method that encodes `"chat_id:thread_id"` for outbound sends
- Backward-compatible serde: `#[serde(default)]` so existing JSON without `thread_id` deserializes as `None`

**Session isolation** (`crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs`):
- `default_channel_session_key()` appends `:thread_id` when present → `telegram:bot:chat:thread`
- `resolve_channel_session()` passes `thread_id` through to DB lookup
- `set_active_session()` calls include `thread_id`

**Database** (`crates/sessions/src/metadata.rs` + migration):
- `channel_sessions` table gets `thread_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` in the primary key
- Migration recreates the table to update the PK (SQLite limitation)
- `get_active_session()` / `set_active_session()` accept `thread_id: Option<&str>`

**Telegram inbound** (`crates/telegram/src/handlers.rs`):
- `extract_thread_id()` reads `msg.thread_id` from teloxide's `Message`
- All 4 `ChannelReplyTarget` constructions now include `thread_id`
- Callback queries extract thread_id from the associated message
- Keyboard/card functions (`send_sessions_keyboard`, `send_agent_keyboard`, etc.) parse composite `to` and apply `message_thread_id`

**Telegram outbound** (`crates/telegram/src/outbound.rs`):
- `parse_chat_target()` splits `"chat_id:thread_id"` composite strings
- All send methods (`send_text`, `send_html`, `send_text_silent`, `send_media`, `send_location`, `send_stream`) parse the composite `to` and set `message_thread_id` on Telegram API calls
- All media sends (photo, document, voice, audio, venue, location) include thread routing

**Other channels** (Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, MS Teams):
- All `ChannelReplyTarget` constructions get `thread_id: None`

**Tests**: New tests for session key with thread_id, `outbound_to()`, serde roundtrip, backward-compatible deserialization, and thread-isolated active sessions.

This adds thread_id awareness throughout the channel pipeline:\n\n- ChannelReplyTarget gains thread_id field (serde-compatible with existing JSON)\n- Session keys include thread_id: telegram:bot:chat:thread\n- channel_sessions DB table adds thread_id to primary key (migration included)\n- Telegram inbound extracts msg.thread_id from teloxide Message\n- Telegram outbound parses composite \"chat_id:thread_id\" addresses and\n  sets message_thread_id on all send requests (text, media, stream, location)\n- Keyboard/card UI functions route to correct forum topic\n\nCloses moltis-org/moltis#442\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Create commit"
}

is defined identically here and in `crates/telegram/src/outbound.rs`, but with different error-handling semantics. The `outbound.rs` version propagates parse errors via `Result\u003c_\u003e` (using `?`), while this version silently substitutes `ChatId(0)` via `unwrap_or(0)` on both the chat-ID and thread-ID paths.\n\n   If a malformed composite address somehow reaches this handler, it will silently send to `ChatId(0)` — a non-existent chat — rather than surfacing the error. This is also a maintenance hazard: any future format change must be applied in two places.\n\n   Consider extracting a shared utility (e.g. `crates/telegram/src/util.rs` or similar) that both files import, using the `Result`-based signature from `outbound.rs`.\n\n\n2. `crates/telegram/src/outbound.rs`, line 1109-1111 ([link](https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/blob/1ced3fc7c17c27359023d32a39a8784bb02e7b9b/crates/telegram/src/outbound.rs#L1109-L1111)) \n\n   \u003ca href=\"#\"\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"P2\" src=\"https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7\" align=\"top\"\u003e\u003c/a\u003e **Typing indicator ignored for forum topics**\n\n   `send_typing` extracts `thread_id` but then discards it (`_thread_id`). The Telegram Bot API's `sendChatAction` supports `message_thread_id` (since Bot API 6.0), so without it the \"typing…\" indicator appears at the chat's top level rather than inside the correct forum topic. ```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/oasis-cattle/crates/telegram/src/topic.rs",
  "content": "//! Forum-topic (thread) routing helpers for Telegram supergroups.\n\nuse teloxide::types::{ChatId, MessageId, ThreadId};\n\nuse crate::Result;\n\n/// Parse a composite `to` address into `(ChatId, Option<ThreadId>)`.\n///\n/// Forum-topic sends encode the thread as `\"chat_id:thread_id\"`.\n/// Plain sends are just `\"chat_id\"`.\npub(crate) fn parse_chat_target(to: &str) -> Result<(ChatId, Option<ThreadId>)> {\n    if let Some((chat_part, thread_part)) = to.split_once(':') {\n        let chat_id = ChatId(chat_part.parse::<i64>()?);\n        let thread_id = thread_part\n            .parse::<i32>()\n            .ok()\n            .map(|id| ThreadId(MessageId(id)));\n        Ok((chat_id, thread_id))\n    } else {\n        Ok((ChatId(to.parse::<i64>()?), None))\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_plain_chat_id() {\n        let (chat, thread) = parse_chat_target(\"12345\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(chat, ChatId(12345));\n        assert!(thread.is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_negative_chat_id() {\n        let (chat, thread) = parse_chat_target(\"-100999\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(chat, ChatId(-100999));\n        assert!(thread.is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_chat_with_thread() {\n        let (chat, thread) = parse_chat_target(\"-100999:42\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(chat, ChatId(-100999));\n        assert_eq!(thread.unwrap().0 .0, 42);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_invalid_chat_id_is_err() {\n        assert!(parse_chat_target(\"not_a_number\").is_err());\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

---
author: greptile-apps
date: 2026-03-28T08:35:08Z
url: https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/pull/498#issuecomment-4147623966
body: <h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR isolates Telegram forum-topic (supergroup thread) sessions by extending `ChannelReplyTarget` with a `thread_id` field and threading that value through every layer — session key generation, DB metadata lookups, and all outbound Telegram API calls — so that each forum topic gets its own session, interrupt bucket, and message history.

**Key changes:**
- `ChannelReplyTarget.thread_id: Option<String>` added with `serde(default)` for backward-compatible deserialization of existing persisted JSON
- `outbound_to()` encodes forum-topic addresses as `\"chat_id:thread_id\"`, consumed by the new `parse_chat_target` helper in `topic.rs`
- SQLite migration recreates `channel_sessions` with `thread_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` added to the primary key; existing rows migrated with an empty string representing \"no thread\"
- All outbound paths (text, media, voice, audio, location, venue, streaming, keyboards, typing indicator) propagate `message_thread_id` to the Telegram Bot API
- All other channel adapters (Discord, Slack, MS Teams, WhatsApp, HTTP) set `thread_id: None` — no behavioral change
- 696 tests pass; new unit tests cover the session key format, `outbound_to()`, serde round-trip, and backward-compatible deserialization

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

Safe to merge — all outbound paths are updated, the migration is correct, backward compatibility is preserved, and 696 tests pass.

The only finding is a P2 style issue: `parse_chat_target` silently converts an unparseable thread ID to `None` instead of propagating an error. In practice, thread IDs are always valid i32 values produced by `extract_thread_id`, so this path is unreachable under normal operation. No data loss, security, or correctness risk is present in the changed code.

crates/telegram/src/topic.rs — minor asymmetric error handling in `parse_chat_target`.

| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| crates/telegram/src/topic.rs | N

body: Addressed both Greptile findings in 5c8a15a8:

1. **Duplicate `parse_chat_target`** — extracted into `crates/telegram/src/topic.rs` as a shared utility with proper `Result<_, ParseIntError>` error propagation. Both `outbound.rs` and `handlers.rs` now import from there. The handler-side wrapper (`parse_chat_target_lossy`) uses `unwrap_or` only for UI helpers where error propagation is impractical.

2. **Typing indicator in forum topics** — `send_typing` now sets `message_thread_id` on `sendChatAction` so the typing indicator appears in the correct forum topic. Added `SendChatActionSetters` import.

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



--
| crates/telegram/src/topic.rs | New module implementing `parse_chat_target` to decode composite `"chat_id:thread_id"` addresses; chat_id parse errors propagate but thread_id errors are silently swallowed. |
| crates/sessions/migrations/20260328100000_channel_sessions_thread_id.sql | Recreates `channel_sessions` table with `thread_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` added to the primary key; migrates existing rows with `''` as thread_id via `INSERT OR IGNORE` before dropping the old table. |
| crates/sessions/src/metadata.rs | Adds `thread_id: Option<&str>` to `get_active_session` / `set_active_session`; maps `None` to empty string `''` to match the DB default, keeping non-forum sessions backward compatible. |
| crates/channels/src/plugin.rs | Adds optional `thread_id` field to `ChannelReplyTarget` (serde-default for backward compat) and new `outbound_to()` helper that encodes `"chat_id:thread_id"` for forum topics; well-tested. |
| crates/telegram/src/outbound.rs | All outbound paths (text, html, media, voice, audio, location, venue, stream) now call `parse_chat_target(to)` and propagate `thread_id` to every Telegram API request via `message_thread_id`. |
| crates/telegram/src/handlers.rs | Inbound handler extracts `thread_id` from messages, populates `ChannelReplyTarget`, and routes keyboards/cards to the correct forum topic via `outbound_to()`; callback queries correctly extract thread_id from the embedded message. |

     1→//! Forum-topic (thread) routing helpers for Telegram supergroups.
     2→
     3→use std::num::ParseIntError;
     4→
     5→use teloxide::types::{ChatId, MessageId, ThreadId};
     6→
     7→/// Parse a composite `to` address into `(ChatId, Option<ThreadId>)`.
     8→///
     9→/// Forum-topic sends encode the thread as `"chat_id:thread_id"`.
    10→/// Plain sends are just `"chat_id"`.
    11→pub(crate) fn parse_chat_target(to: &str) -> Result<(ChatId, Option<ThreadId>), ParseIntError> {
    12→    if let Some((chat_part, thread_part)) = to.split_once(':') {
    13→        let chat_id = ChatId(chat_part.parse::<i64>()?);
    14→        let thread_id = thread_part
    15→            .parse::<i32>()
    16→            .ok()
    17→            .map(|id| ThreadId(MessageId(id)));
    18→        Ok((chat_id, thread_id))
    19→    } else {
    20→        Ok((ChatId(to.parse::<i64>()?), None))
    21→    }
    22→}
    23→
